# Episode — `/insights`

**Status:** draft, not reviewed
**Cast:** Dev 1 (asks, and shares), Dev 2 (says it first), Dev 3 (the payoff), Dev 1's burnt-out AI
**Bucket:** cost → outro is the credits counter
**Ending engine:** **callback** — he pastes the link in the channel in the first minute, before he has read it. Paid off in the last panel by the one person who reads everything.

| # | Direction | Line |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **WIDE — two desks.** Dev 2 wrecked, coffee, talking past Dev 1 rather than to him. Dev 1 right-of-centre so the clear space falls left. | **DEV 2** "ran /insights on my sessions last night. brutal." |
| 2 | **Close on Dev 1.** Polite face. | **DEV 1** "Right. Insights. Yeah." |
| 3 | Framing identical to 2. Mouth closes into the small smile. Thought caption. | *I have no idea what that means.* |
| 4 | His own desk. Leaning toward the mic, hand cupped, checking nobody is listening. | **DEV 1** "what's /insights" |
| 5 | The screen. AI mark, centred. | **AI** "It reads every session you've had with me. Then it writes down how you work." |
| 6 | Dev 1 straightens up. This sounds like good news. The want, said out loud, as a request. | **DEV 1** "Run it. I want something to show at three." |
| 7 | **The screen doing the thing.** A counter climbing, a bar filling: `612 sessions · 344 analysed`. No dialogue. | — |
| 8 | Screen. | **AI** "Done. There's a link." |
| 9 | Dev 1 typing fast, pleased with himself. The team channel, his message going up. **This is the plant.** | **DEV 1** "Ran this on my own sessions. Happy to walk everyone through it at three." |
| 10 | He sits back. Opens it for the first time. | **DEV 1** "Right. Let's see." |
| 11 | Screen — the first section, `WHAT'S WORKING`. Two flattering lines. Cut to him, genuinely relaxing. | **DEV 1** "That's fair. That's accurate." |
| 12 | Screen — he scrolls. `WHERE THINGS GO WRONG`. Three categories, and they keep going below the fold. | — |
| 13 | **Close on Dev 1.** Hand on the mouse. Scrolling slower. No dialogue. | — |
| 14 | Screen — the suggestion box. `Add to CLAUDE.md:` *"Explain it like I have not done this before."* Below it, small: `You have asked for this in 41 sessions.` | — |
| 15 | Close on Dev 1. Nothing moves. The polite face, aimed at nothing. | — |
| 16 | **Framing identical to panel 6.** He runs it again. The screen returns the same line. | **AI** "It's the same sessions." |
| 17 | Dev 3 has arrived at his shoulder. Glasses. Not unkind. Holding nothing. | **DEV 3** "I read the whole thing." |
| 18 | Framing identical to panel 2. Polite face. | — |
| 19 | Stamp, holding alone. | `/INSIGHTS · CLEARED` |
| 20 | **OUTRO.** Stamp holds. Below it, small and lit: `Credits remaining: 0`. | **DEV 1** "…hey. What happened to my AI credits?" |

## Notes

**The want, and why he shares before reading.** Panel 5 tells him the report is *about him*, and he pastes it anyway in panel 9 — because the thing he wants is to be the person who is ahead on this, and the cheapest way to look ahead is to be seen running it. Panel 11 is flattering, which retroactively makes the gamble look smart. That is what makes panel 12 cost something.

**Teach, in three moves.** Panel 5 says what it does. Panel 7 demonstrates the scale — it went through everything you have ever done. Panel 14 gives the part nobody tells you: the suggestions come from the things you have said to it over and over, so the report knows what you keep not learning.

**Panel 14 is the hero frame.** The whole episode is one screenshot: a polite suggestion box asking a senior engineer to write down, in a file his team can read, that he needs things explained from scratch. It has to be readable at a glance, before anyone parses the small line under it.

**Panel 16 earns the bucket.** He re-runs it hoping for a different report. That is the credits going, and it is the most in-character thing he does all episode.

**Why callback, not the loop.** Three of the five existing scripts end structurally. `/insights` has nothing cyclical in it — the term is a one-shot act of looking at yourself — so the device would be borrowed rather than earned. The link goes up in panel 9 and comes back in panel 17, in someone else's mouth.

**Panel 17 is the punchline and it must be flat.** Dev 3's line is supportive. He is not needling him; he has simply read it, the way he reads everything. No reaction shot on Dev 3 — cut straight to panel 18.

## Flags

**⚠ Test 2 is this term's real weak point, and it is weaker than `skill`'s was.** `/insights` is new enough that a senior person is not embarrassed not to know it, which is the engine the series runs on. My view: it still works, but only if you accept a **deliberate variant of the premise** — the shame is not in the word, it is in the output. "I don't know what that is" becomes "I don't want to know what it would say about me," which is the same status anxiety pointed one step further in. The signature top beat (panels 2 and 3) stays intact either way. If Anandhi or Prashasti won't take the variant, this term should be parked, not softened.

**Caption vs VO on the term.** Captions read `/insights`; the VO says "slash insights". They will not match word-for-word in the caption reveal. Needs your call before recording — I'd keep the slash on screen, because the get-started moment is someone typing it.

**Spot-check before drawing** (the class of claim that broke v1 of `loop`):
1. The report URL is genuinely shareable with a colleague, not just a local HTML file. The whole ending hangs on this. I have the product's own string — *"Your shareable insights report is ready"* — plus a storage-backend write path, which is good evidence but not a test.
2. A re-run does cost something. Per-session facets are cached against the transcript mtime, so a second run is cheaper than the first. The outro is the series' fixed device rather than a stated claim, so I don't think it's exposed — but panel 16 should not have the AI say anything about money.
3. `612 sessions · 344 analysed` in panel 7 should match the shape of a real run on a real machine. Run it on yours and use the true ratio.

**Bucket alternation:** `loop` was cost, `graph` and `skill` are both speed. Cost is due, so the running order supports this one.

Want me to commit it as `comics/scripts/insights.md` (status: draft) so the repo has the frozen copy, or hold it here until the premise variant is settled?

# Episode — `/insights`

**Status:** draft, not reviewed
**Cast:** Dev 1 (asks, and shares), Dev 2 (says it first), Dev 3 (the payoff), Dev 1's burnt-out AI
**Ending engine:** loop by accumulation — the report counts his sessions, and asking about the report makes another one. No bucket, no outro card.

| # | Direction | Line |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **WIDE — two desks.** Dev 2 wrecked, coffee, talking past Dev 1 rather than to him. Dev 1 right-of-centre so the clear space falls left. | **DEV 2** "ran /insights on my sessions last night. brutal." |
| 2 | **Close on Dev 1.** Polite face. | **DEV 1** "Right. Insights. Yeah." |
| 3 | Framing identical to 2. Mouth closes into the small smile. Thought caption. | *I have no idea what that means.* |
| 4 | His own desk. Leaning toward the mic, hand cupped, checking nobody is listening. | **DEV 1** "what's /insights" |
| 5 | The screen. AI mark, centred. | **AI** "It reads every session you've had with me. Then it writes down how you work." |
| 6 | Dev 1 straightens up. This sounds like good news. The want, said out loud, as a request. | **DEV 1** "Run it. I want something to show at three." |
| 7 | **The screen doing the thing.** A counter climbing, a bar filling: `612 sessions · 344 analysed`. No dialogue. **Remember this plate.** | — |
| 8 | Screen. | **AI** "Done. There's a link." |
| 9 | Dev 1 typing fast, pleased with himself. The team channel, his message going up. **This is the plant.** | **DEV 1** "Ran this on my own sessions. Happy to walk everyone through it at three." |
| 10 | Screen — the first section, `WHAT'S WORKING`. Two flattering lines. Cut to him, relaxing. | **DEV 1** "That's fair. That's accurate." |
| 11 | Screen — he scrolls. `WHERE THINGS GO WRONG`. Three categories, and they keep going below the fold. | — |
| 12 | **Close on Dev 1.** Hand on the mouse. Scrolling slower. No dialogue. | — |
| 13 | Screen — the suggestion box. `Add to CLAUDE.md:` *"Explain it like I have not done this before."* Below it, small: `You have asked for this in 41 sessions.` | — |
| 14 | Close on Dev 1. Nothing moves. The polite face, aimed at nothing. | — |
| 15 | Dev 3 has arrived at his shoulder. Glasses. Not unkind. Holding nothing. | **DEV 3** "I read the whole thing." |
| 16 | Framing identical to panel 2. Polite face. | — |
| 17 | Stamp, holding alone. | `/INSIGHTS · CLEARED` |
| 18 | **Framing identical to panel 4.** Back to the mic. Hand cupped. Smaller than the first time. | **DEV 1** "Sorry, one more thing and then I'll leave you alone." |
| 19 | **Panel 7's own plate, unchanged.** The scan running again. The counter reads `613 sessions · 1 analysing`. **HARD CUT to panel 1.** | — |

## Notes

**Why this loop and not the handback.** `loop`, `graph` and `skill` all end by returning a line to a person. This term has its own cycle built in and it is funnier: the report is a record of your sessions, so the act of being ashamed of the report goes into the record. Nobody says it. The only thing that tells you is `612 → 613`, which is why panel 7 has to be held long enough to read.

**Panel 18 is the whole series in one line.** "Sorry, one more thing and then I'll leave you alone" is his canon line — a lead apologising to software. He has just been handed a document saying he asks the same thing forty-one times, and his response is to ask again, quietly, politely. Do not add a reaction to it.

**Panel 13 is still the hero frame.** A polite suggestion box asking a senior engineer to write down, in a file his team can read, that he needs things explained from scratch.

**The re-run beat is gone.** It only existed to earn the credits counter. Without the bucket it was a dry beat competing with panel 18, which is the better version of the same idea.

**Production, if you want the EP003-style seamless loop.** As written, the last frame is a screen and the first is a wide, so the join is a hard cut like every other cut in the film. To make the last frame pixel-identical to the first instead: put the counter on panel 18's screen behind him, and make panel 19 panel 1's plate with Dev 2 mid-sentence, his line split across the join in one recording. My view is don't — the counter needs to be the last thing you see, and it cannot be if it is competing with a person talking.

## Two things this changes outside the script

**The skill file now disagrees with practice.** `skills/comic-script-writer.md` still says every episode is filed under speed / cost / quality and closes with one of three fixed outros, and "fixed is the point." If buckets are done, that section and the *"Close every script with the stamp panel, then the bucket's outro panel"* line in **Output format** need rewriting, or the next person to pick up a script writes a credits counter. Say the word and I'll do it in one pass — I won't touch the skill files without you asking.

**Keep the stamp, though.** With the outro gone it is the only recognition furniture left: it costs about a second and a half and it is what tells a returning viewer this was an episode about a word rather than a sketch. If you want that gone too, panel 16 becomes the last beat before the loop coda and the episode ends on the polite face — workable, but then the series has no repeating device at all outside the top two panels, and I'd argue that's one subtraction too many.

The `/insights` accuracy flags from before still stand — the shareable link and the real `sessions · analysed` ratio both want a spot-check on your own machine before anything gets drawn.

Your version is better than mine and I'd build on it, not around it. Three reasons: the AI reading the report aloud while he tries to argue with it is a live scene where mine was a man scrolling; the escalation is structural instead of a list; and the loop finally has a payload — the machine tells him to ask a human, a human immediately asks him something, and he lies. One thing your outline settles for free: **AJ is Dev 4**, so his AI is the absurdly formal one. A legal-department voice reading out his failings, calling him sir, is a much funnier instrument than the burnt-out register I had. And it means the `bruh` card is on the table.

# Episode — `/insights`

**Status:** draft, not reviewed
**Cast:** **AJ** (Dev 4, cap, feet up) and his AI (absurdly formal) · **Sameer** (Dev 2, wrecked, ran it last night) · **Peter** (Dev 1, the lead, walks in at the end)
**Ending engine:** loop — the machine's last suggestion is "ask a colleague," a colleague immediately asks him something, and he bluffs. Sameer's line is one recording split across the cut.
**No bucket, no outro card, no stamp.** See the note below on the stamp.

| # | Direction | Line |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **WIDE — AJ's desk.** Sameer standing over it, coffee, wrecked. AJ, cap, feet up, phone in hand, barely looking. AJ right-of-centre so the clear space falls left. | **SAMEER** "hey AJ, hope your AI was less brutal than mine with /insights." |
| 2 | **Close on AJ.** The bluff, fully committed. | **AJ** "ohh /insights? nah nah, bet mine was worse" |
| 3 | Framing identical to 2. Mouth closes into the small smile. Thought caption. | *I have no idea what that means.* |
| 4 | AJ alone, leaning in toward his screen for the first time. Feet still up. | **AJ** "yo what's /insights" |
| 5 | The screen. AI mark, centred. | **AI** "A report on your working patterns, sir. I review every session we have had and write them up." |
| 6 | **THE WANT.** AJ, unbothered, already reaching for the keyboard. | **AJ** "cool. show me mine" |
| 7 | **The screen doing the thing.** A counter climbing, a bar filling: `612 sessions · 344 analysed`. No dialogue. | — |
| 8 | Screen. First card. | **AI** "Section one. Strengths. You are unafraid to attempt tasks outside your experience." |
| 9 | AJ, pleased, settling back in. The next card lands on him mid-word. | **AJ** "fair fair i know-" |
| 10 | Screen. Second card. | **AI** "You frequently ask me to summarise documents you were sent, and then reply as though you had read them." |
| 11 | AJ. **The feet come down.** | **AJ** "hey i don't do that-" |
| 12 | Screen. Third card. | **AI** "On Tuesday you deleted a failing test rather than repair it. You then requested a test that passes." |
| 13 | AJ standing now, hands up at the monitor. | **AJ** "yo yo you need to calm dow-" |
| 14 | Screen. The suggestion box. `Sessions most often go wrong after this instruction:` / `"whatever, go"` / `used in 41 sessions` | — |
| 15 | AJ, both hands in his hair, arguing with furniture. | **AJ** "i thought you were supposed to know all that and pick for-" |
| 16 | Screen. One word, lowercase, alone on the monitor. Hold it. | **AI** "bruh." |
| 17 | Screen. The formal register snaps straight back. | **AI** "Apologies, sir. One final suggestion to improve prompt quality. Consider asking a colleague." |
| 18 | **WIDE — across the room.** Peter and Sameer. AJ small in the background, still standing at his desk. Peter does not ask AJ. | **PETER** "why is he freaking out?" / **SAMEER** "he ran /insights. first time." |
| 19 | Same framing. Sameer is already turning back toward AJ's desk. | **SAMEER** "hang on. let me actually ask him." |
| 20 | **Panel 1's own plate, unchanged.** Sameer breaks mid-word. **HARD CUT to panel 1.** | **SAMEER** "hey AJ, hope your AI was less-" |

## Notes

**Why he can't finish a sentence.** Four interruptions in a row is a tic unless something is causing them. The cause is on screen: **the report advances itself.** He never scrolls once. Every time he opens his mouth the next card lands, which is also the only way a written report can interrupt a person. Panels 9, 11, 13 and 15 all cut on the consonant, mid-word, and the card is already up when we return.

**The escalation has to be graded or the scene is flat.** Panel 8 is genuinely nice, so panel 9 is genuine agreement. Panel 10 is deniable — that's why he denies it. Panel 12 is not deniable, has a date, and has a consequence. Panel 14 is the one that can't be argued with because it's a number and it's his own words.

**`bruh` is spent here and only here.** It is the AI worn down into speaking his language, and it works because it sits between two maximally corporate lines. Panel 17's apology is the bigger laugh of the two — do not cut it for length.

**Peter asks Sameer, not AJ.** He is standing fifteen feet from a man who can answer the question and he asks the other guy instead. Nobody flags it. That is the whole series in one line of staging and it costs nothing.

**AJ's arc is in his legs.** Feet up (1, 4, 6) → feet down (11) → standing (13, 15, 18). He is the character defined by being unbothered, so the episode only works if you can see the rule breaking. He is not upset that something is broken — he is upset that it's about him, which is the one thing that's never been about him before.

**The loop.** Panel 20 is panel 1's plate, pixel-identical, so nothing moves across the join except the voice. Record Sameer's line **once** and split it at "less-" — two takes and his pitch will step across the cut. Second delivery is knowing rather than friendly: he watched what that question does and he's doing it again.

## Flags

**The stamp doesn't survive this ending.** Last time I argued to keep it as the one recognition device left after buckets. That holds when an episode ends on a card; it doesn't hold here, because the value of this ending is a seam you can't see, and a `/INSIGHTS · CLEARED` card two seconds before the join is the one thing guaranteed to break it. Ending on the loop is worth more than the furniture. Your call, but I'd drop it for this one rather than for the series.

**Sameer isn't in the cast file.** `characters/cast.md` has AJ (Dev 4) and Peter (Dev 1) named through `graph` and `remote-control`, but Dev 2 has never been named on screen. If Sameer is him, that name needs adding to the file before the next script, or the next writer invents a second one. Also worth the same check `subagent.md` flags for Peter: if Sameer is a real colleague's name, change it.

**Accuracy — one class of line to police.** `/insights` only ever reads *your own* sessions, so it cannot compare AJ to anyone. Every card must be self-referential. Panel 8 is written to that rule deliberately ("outside your experience", not "faster than the team"). If anyone reaches for a ranking line in review, it's wrong.

**Spot-check before drawing:** the real `sessions · analysed` ratio from a run on your own machine, for panel 7.

Want me to write this to `comics/scripts/insights.md` as a draft, and separately strip the bucket section out of `skills/comic-script-writer.md` so the rules match what we're actually doing now?

# Episode — `/insights`

**Status:** draft, not reviewed
**Cast:** **AJ** (Dev 4, cap, feet up) and his AI (absurdly formal) · **Sameer** (Dev 2, wrecked, ran it last night) · **Peter** (Dev 1, the lead, walks in at the end)
**Ending engine:** loop — the machine's last suggestion is "ask a colleague," a colleague immediately asks him something, and he bluffs. Sameer's line is one recording split across the cut.
**No stamp, no outro card.** The film ends on the loop.

| # | Direction | Line |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **WIDE — AJ's desk.** Sameer standing over it, coffee, wrecked. AJ, cap, feet up, phone in hand, barely looking. AJ right-of-centre so the clear space falls left. | **SAMEER** "hey AJ, hope your AI was less brutal than mine with /insights." |
| 2 | **Close on AJ.** The bluff, fully committed. | **AJ** "ohh /insights? nah nah, bet mine was worse" |
| 3 | Framing identical to 2. Mouth closes into the small smile. Thought caption. | *I have no idea what that means.* |
| 4 | AJ alone, leaning in toward his screen for the first time. Feet still up. | **AJ** "yo what's /insights" |
| 5 | The screen. AI mark, centred. | **AI** "A report on your working patterns, sir. I review every session we have had and write them up." |
| 6 | **THE WANT.** AJ, unbothered, already reaching for the keyboard. | **AJ** "cool. show me mine" |
| 7 | **The screen doing the thing.** A counter climbing, a bar filling: `612 sessions · 344 analysed`. No dialogue. | — |
| 8 | Screen. First card. | **AI** "Section one. Strengths. You are unafraid to attempt tasks outside your experience." |
| 9 | AJ, pleased, settling back in. The next card lands on him mid-word. | **AJ** "fair fair i know-" |
| 10 | Screen. Second card. | **AI** "You frequently ask me to summarise documents you were sent, and then reply as though you had read them." |
| 11 | AJ. **The feet come down.** | **AJ** "hey i don't do that-" |
| 12 | Screen. Third card. | **AI** "On Tuesday you deleted a failing test rather than repair it. You then requested a test that passes." |
| 13 | AJ standing now, hands up at the monitor. | **AJ** "yo yo you need to calm dow-" |
| 14 | Screen. Card title `SUGGESTION BOX` sets first. The AI reads the line, stops, and quotes him. On the beat after, `used in 41 sessions` pops in under the quote with a *ding*. | **AI** "Sessions most often go wrong after this instruction." *(pause)* "whatever." · *ding* `used in 41 sessions` |
| 15 | AJ, both hands in his hair, arguing with furniture. Cut off mid-word again. | **AJ** "i thought you were supposed to know all that and pick for-" |
| 16 | Screen. Same flat register it has held all episode. | **AI** "One final suggestion to improve prompt quality, sir. Consider asking a colleague." |
| 17 | **WIDE — across the room.** Peter and Sameer. AJ small in the background, still on his feet at his desk, still going. His voice is there but not a word of it is intelligible. | **PETER** "why is he freaking out?" / **SAMEER** "he ran /insights. first time." · *(AJ, under)* |
| 18 | Same framing. Sameer is already turning back toward AJ's desk. AJ still going behind him. | **SAMEER** "hang on. let me actually ask him." · *(AJ, under)* |
| 19 | **Panel 1's own plate, unchanged.** Sameer breaks mid-word. **HARD CUT to panel 1.** | **SAMEER** "hey AJ, hope your AI was less-" |

## Notes

**Panel 14 is the only panel where the screen out-times the voice.** Everywhere else the card lands and the AI reads it. Here the title sets, the AI stops dead before quoting him, and the count arrives *after* — a third beat, on the ding, when he has already heard the worst of it. One word, in its voice, is the whole friction section.

**The quote is one word.** `"whatever."` — not "whatever, go." The single word is worse, because it reads as the entire instruction he gave, forty-one times.

**Why he can't finish a sentence.** Four interruptions (9, 11, 13, 15) need a cause or they read as a tic. The cause is on screen: **the report advances itself.** He never scrolls once. Every time he opens his mouth the next card lands. Cut on the consonant, mid-word, and the card is already up when we come back.

**The escalation is graded.** 8 is genuinely nice, so 9 is genuine agreement. 10 is deniable, which is why he denies it. 12 has a date and a consequence. 14 can't be argued with because it's his own word and a number.

**The register never breaks.** No `bruh` anywhere in this episode. It reads him his own failings, gets shouted at, and its last line is still "sir." That's the coldest available version and it leaves the card unspent.

**AJ's arc is in his legs.** Feet up (1, 4, 6) → feet down (11) → standing (13, 15, 17, 18). He is the character defined by being unbothered, so the episode only works if you can see the rule breaking. Nothing is broken here except him.

**Peter asks Sameer, not AJ.** He is standing fifteen feet from the man who can answer and he asks the other guy. Nobody flags it.

## Production

**AJ's bickering bed (17–18).** Record it as one continuous ad-lib pass, not lines — filtered and low, no intelligible words, ducked under Peter and Sameer. He is still arguing with a monitor that stopped replying two panels ago.

**Kill the bed dead on the cut to 19.** The silence is what tells you the film has gone back to the start. If it bleeds across, the loop reads as a continuity error instead of a loop.

**Panel 19 is panel 1's plate, pixel-identical — AJ is feet-up and calm again.** That contradicts the story state on purpose; it's the loop resetting, same as EP003's last frame. Flag it in the storyboard or someone will "fix" it by drawing him standing.

**Record Sameer's line once and split it at "less-".** Two takes and his pitch steps across the cut. Second delivery is knowing, not friendly — he has watched what that question does and he's doing it again.

## Flags

**Sameer isn't in `characters/cast.md`.** AJ and Peter are named through `graph` and `remote-control`; Dev 2 has never been named. Needs adding before the next script, and the same check `subagent.md` makes for Peter — if Sameer is a real colleague, change it.

**Accuracy:** `/insights` only reads *your own* sessions, so it can never compare AJ to anyone. Panel 8 is written to that deliberately. If a ranking line turns up in review, it's wrong.

**Spot-check before drawing:** a real `sessions · analysed` ratio from a run on your machine, for panel 7.

## ASSISTANT
Read all four. Harshit's Slack message is the useful one — it's the `/insights` system prompt, and it changes the episode.

Three things in it I didn't have:

- The report's own brief says **"Do NOT be sycophantic. Praise only what the data supports."** The machine isn't taking shots at AJ. It was told not to flatter him, and it is obeying. That single fact is the fix for "too intentional."
- **Section 7b must output a complete, copyable SKILL.md with frontmatter** — not advice, a file. That's exactly your "fetch a skill file so it stops assuming," and it's in the product, not invented.
- Every friction example must cite **the session, the file, how many messages were spent, and how it resolved.** Numbers, not opinions.

So the rewrite: the roast drops from three cards to two, the zinger (`"Consider asking a colleague"`) is gone entirely — that was the AI aiming at him, which is the thing that read as intentional — and the back half becomes the report *fixing* him. The comedy moves from what it says about AJ to what it hands him.

---

# Episode — `/insights` (v2)

**Cast:** **AJ** (Dev 4) and his AI (absurdly formal) · **Sameer** (Dev 2) · **Peter** (Dev 1)
**Ending engine:** loop — Sameer's line is one recording split across the cut. Panel 20 is panel 1's plate.
**What changed:** the AI never aims at him. It reads sections in order, quotes his own words back, and then writes him the file he should have written himself.

| # | Direction | Line |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **WIDE — AJ's desk.** Sameer standing over it, coffee, wrecked. AJ, cap, feet up, phone, barely looking. | **SAMEER** "hey AJ, hope your AI was less brutal than mine with /insights." |
| 2 | Close on AJ. The bluff, fully committed. | **AJ** "ohh /insights? nah nah, bet mine was worse" |
| 3 | Framing identical to 2. Mouth closes into the small smile. Thought caption. | *I have no idea what that means.* |
| 4 | AJ alone, leaning in toward his screen for the first time. Feet still up. | **AJ** "yo what's /insights" |
| 5 | The screen. AI mark, centred. | **AI** "It reads every session we have had, sir, and writes up how you work." |
| 6 | **THE WANT.** AJ, unbothered, reaching for the keyboard. | **AJ** "cool. show me mine" |
| 7 | **The screen doing the thing.** Counter climbing, bar filling: `94 sessions · 1,194 prompts · 124 commits · 22 days`. No dialogue. | — |
| 8 | Screen. Section card, and it is genuinely nice. | **AI** "Section five. What works. You attempt things far outside your experience, and you are not discouraged when they fail." |
| 9 | AJ, pleased, settling back in. The next card lands on him mid-word. | **AJ** "fair fair i know-" |
| 10 | Screen. Friction card, with the citation the report always carries. | **AI** "Section six. Friction. On Tuesday you deleted a failing test rather than repair it, then requested a test that passes. Nine messages were spent on this." |
| 11 | AJ. **The feet come down.** | **AJ** "hey i don't do that-" |
| 12 | Screen. The diagnosis. The AI stops, then quotes him. A beat later `used in 41 of 94 sessions` pops in under the quote on a *ding*, not spoken. | **AI** "Your sessions go wrong most often after a one word instruction." *(pause)* "whatever." · *ding* `used in 41 of 94 sessions` |
| 13 | AJ standing, hands up at the monitor. | **AJ** "yo yo you need to calm dow-" |
| 14 | **THE TURN.** Screen. Same flat register. It is not arguing with him, it is on the next section. | **AI** "Section seven. Suggestions. I have written you a skill file, sir. It tells me what you mean when you say whatever." |
| 15 | **HERO PANEL — the skill file, full screen.** Frontmatter and four numbered lines. AJ reads it standing up. His line comes late, quiet, after the beat. | `---`<br>`name: whatever`<br>`description: what AJ means when he says whatever. read before assuming.`<br>`---`<br>`1. Do not pick an approach. Ask which one.`<br>`2. He has not read the ticket. Summarise it first.`<br>`3. Never delete a test to make a run pass.`<br>`4. Say what you changed. One line. His words.`<br>· **AJ** "...yeah. yeah that's what i meant" |
| 16 | AJ sits back down. Types one word, the same way he always has. | **AJ** "whatever, go" |
| 17 | **THE DEMONSTRATION.** Screen. It does not guess. It asks the one question, he answers in one word, and a progress line completes clean. | **AI** "Two approaches, sir. Fast, or correct?" · **AJ** "correct" |
| 18 | AJ out of his chair, delighted, yelling at the whole room. Nobody has ever seen him care about anything. | **AJ** "YO. YO IT ASKED ME A QUESTION" |
| 19 | Screen, behind him, ignored, still working through the report on schedule. It labels this section itself. | **AI** "Section nine. Fun ending." · `LOCAL MAN DELETES TEST TO STOP TEST FAILING. TEST STOPS FAILING.` |
| 20 | **WIDE — across the room.** Peter and Sameer. AJ small in the background, still going, not a word of it intelligible. Peter does not ask AJ. Sameer is already turning back toward the desk on his second line. | **PETER** "why is he freaking out?"<br>**SAMEER** "he ran /insights. first time."<br>**SAMEER** "hang on. let me actually ask him." |
| 21 | **Panel 1's own plate, unchanged.** Sameer breaks mid-word. Background voice cut dead on the join. **HARD CUT to panel 1.** | **SAMEER** "hey AJ, hope your AI was less-" |

## What fixes the "too intentional" problem

**Three rules, and they're all in the product's own brief.**

1. **The AI never answers AJ.** It doesn't cut him off as a comeback — it advances because a report has sections and it is reading them in order. It has not heard him. Cut on the consonant in 9, 11 and 13, but never cut *to* a reaction shot of the screen: there is no reaction, that's the point.
2. **No line is an opinion.** Every one is a date, a count, a citation, or AJ's own word quoted back. The command is instructed not to be sycophantic; the machine is being accurate and has no idea that accuracy is the joke.
3. **Panel 19 is the proof it isn't trying.** It calls the most brutal line in the episode *"Fun ending"* — because that section is genuinely called that. A machine that labels its own knife as the fun part is not being sarcastic on purpose.

The old closing line, *"Consider asking a colleague,"* is deleted. That one was aimed at him, and it was the tell.

## What makes the power land

**Panel 15 is now the hero, not the friction card.** The thing nobody expects from a usage report is that it hands you a working file. Four lines that AJ would never have written, derived from forty-one sessions of his own laziness, and they're *right* — which is why his line is "that's what i meant" rather than a protest. He isn't shamed into changing. He didn't change at all. The machine changed.

**Panel 17 is the whole argument in one exchange.** He types the same lazy word he always types. The difference is one question. Nothing magic happens; it asks instead of assuming, and the run is clean first time.

**Panel 18 is the emotional beat the episode was missing.** The kid who is unbothered by everything is out of his chair over a question. That's what the reviewer means by showing the power — not a better number, a person who suddenly cares.

**The bluff now pays off twice.** He lies at the top to look like he'd already run it. He ends up with something genuinely good, and on the loop Sameer asks him again — and we know exactly how he'll answer.

## Flags

**⚠ Spot-check "Section nine. Fun ending" before drawing — the button depends on it.** It's in the system prompt Harshit shared, but that's his March variant, and I couldn't find a matching section in the current build's strings (the ones I can see run to *On the Horizon*). If it's gone, the headline still works as the report's closing line with no section label, but the "Fun ending" wording is what makes panel 19 land, so it's worth one live run to confirm.

**The other section names are safe.** *What works*, *Friction*, *Suggestions*, the skill file with frontmatter, the friction citation with a message count, and the CLAUDE.md-and-skills split all come straight from the shared prompt and match the shipped build.

**Numbers in panels 7 and 12 should be yours.** `94 / 1,194 / 124 / 22 days` is the shape of a real report, taken from the reference. Run it on your machine and use your own, including the real ratio for "41 of 94."

**One thing I'd watch in review:** `skill` (the written episode) also ends on a thing that plainly works. Different mechanism and a different character, so I don't think they collide, but if both get made, this one should ship first — it's the one where the working thing is the point rather than the setup for a fall.

You're right on both. I read two of the three artifacts, took their auto-summaries at face value, and built on one line from Harshit's prompt. Here's what's actually in them, then the fix.

## What the references actually say

**Harshit's `/insights` explainer (the one I hadn't opened) — this is the mechanism:**
it reads ~30 days of local logs from `~/.claude/`, filters out short sessions, chunks them, summarises each one, caches those summaries, and then **a second pass compares the summaries against each other across sessions.** That cross-session comparison is the whole product. It caps at ~50 sessions, and it costs less than one heavy coding session. It also has a real failure mode: on his machine, 50 of 50 recent sessions were automated watcher pings, which pushed all the actual work out of the window.

**The two real reports** are not roasts. They're diagnostics, and the honest part is **user-side**:

- Headers are `1,194 prompts across 94 sessions (50 analyzed)`, with stat tiles for prompts, commits, lines, files, days, prompts/day.
- Friction arrives as **tag counts**: `Wrong approach 18`, `Buggy code 16`. Not sentences — bars.
- "How You Use Claude Code" hands you a **type**: *"You are a debugging-heavy, iterative user."*
- **Top Tools Used** counts include `AskUserQuestion 7` — how often Claude asked *you* something.
- The user-side finding, in the report's own words: *"Claude often lacks enough context about what you've already ruled out… which means it wastes cycles on approaches that were never going to work."*

## Why the last draft failed the teach

The teach was "it reads your sessions and tells you how you work," which is a definition, not a mechanism. Nobody learns anything from it. **The real teach is: one session cannot see itself. Fifty can.** AJ inside any single session is fine — "whatever, go" works often enough. The thing only exists as a shape across a month, and that shape is what `/insights` is for.

And the reference reports give the joke for free: both Sameer and AJ assume the report is a report card **on the AI**. It isn't. It's about them. That's the episode — not an escalating roast, a genre switch.

On the skill file — dropped as the fix, kept as one of the shapes it offers. A **single line you paste into `CLAUDE.md`** is better here: it's smaller, more people have one, and it's the only version where the viewer can see the whole fix in one frame.

And `sir` is gone. Flagging once: `characters/cast.md` has "Calls him sir" as locked for Dev 4's AI. Tell me if that's dead series-wide or just here.

## How AJ actually uses it — the thing that was missing

**Panel 15 is the answer, and it's one keystroke.** He pastes a line into `CLAUDE.md`. That's the entire fix and it's visible in one frame. He doesn't read it, doesn't understand it, and doesn't change a single habit — panel 15 ends with him typing "whatever, go" exactly as before. **The machine changed, because he installed something the machine wrote about him.**

That's why panel 16 is the payoff and not panel 12. The report didn't make AJ better. It made a rule out of forty one of his worst moments and handed it to him, and now the guessing stops. A viewer can copy that: run it, take the line, paste it.

## Why it stops feeling deliberate

**The AI is not the one delivering bad news — the report is.** It reads section two, then the friction tags, then suggestions, then section eight, in order, because that's the order they're in. It never answers AJ. Panels 9, 11 and 13 are him talking to something that isn't listening.

**Nothing it says is an opinion.** A label, two tag counts, forty one dated prompts, one paste-able line. The only sentence with any edge in it — panel 19 — is the report's *optimistic* section, delivered to a man who is too happy to hear it.

**Panel 12 is the emotional pivot and it's silent on the machine's side.** He goes quiet in 13 because the shape is undeniable, not because he was insulted.

## Notes

**Sameer's opening line does double duty now.** He means "hope yours went easier on you than mine did on me." AJ hears "hope your AI performed better than his." Both readings sit in the same words, and the misunderstanding is what makes him run it. On the loop, the audience knows which one it was.

**Panel 12 uses the pop reveal.** All forty one land together on "forty one," staggered spring, not one at a time — the number has to arrive as a mass, because the point is that no single one of them looked like a problem.

**Panel 19's line is real.** "On the Horizon" is a genuine section in both reference reports, and both use it to describe autonomous workflows that need less from you. I'm not putting words in the product's mouth.

## Flags

**Dropped: "Section nine. Fun ending."** It's in Harshit's March prompt but not in the current build's strings, and I'd rather not hang the button on a section that may not exist. Panel 19 uses "On the horizon," which appears in both real reports.

**Numbers should be yours.** `1,194 / 94 / 50 / 41 / 31 / 19` are shaped from the reference reports. Run it and use your real ones — and note the cap: reports say `94 sessions (50 analyzed)`, which is why panel 7 shows both.

**One more thing worth knowing before the next script:** the reports skew if your recent sessions are automated. Harshit's had 50 of 50 sessions as watcher pings, crowding out his real work. That's a real, funny, and completely different episode — not this one.

That's a real thing about writing rules for these tools, and no character has to say it.

**He changes nothing.** He pastes a rule about himself without reading it, then interrupts the machine with the same word that got him here. The only thing that changed is the file.

**Panel 18 is where the power lands, quietly.** He hesitates because he has never been asked, and the question is one he has to actually answer. That beat is worth more than a celebration and it costs one word.

**Panel 19 is the payoff in his own currency.** Feet down in panel 9, feet up in 19. He's not delighted, he's *free* — two exchanges instead of nine. The report counts messages; the win is fewer of them.

**The loop is now one pixel.** Panel 19 is feet up with a green line; panel 21 is feet up with a red one. Same man, same posture, one colour different, and that's the whole episode. Worth protecting in the storyboard — and worth flagging again that panel 21 resets to red on purpose, as EP003 does.

**Peter asks Sameer why AJ is finished, rather than asking AJ.** Costs nothing, and it's the series in one line of staging.

## Notes

**Panel 18 works because he never closed it.** The report isn't retaliating and it isn't responding to his good mood — it's a document with a section eight, still rendering in a window behind the one he's working in. That's the "unintentional" the feedback asked for: the only line in the episode with a knife in it is the report's *optimistic* section.

**It takes the win off him specifically.** His single contribution all day was answering one question, and the report's next sentence is that soon it won't need to ask. Sixty seconds after "correct..?" was the best thing that happened to him.

**"cool cool cool" is his canon line**, used in the cast file for saying nothing while something burns. This is the one place in the episode to spend it, and the thing burning is him.

**Sameer's panel 1 line is now paid.** He knew what brutal meant, AJ didn't, and by panel 20 he does. On the loop we hear the question a third way.

**One thing this costs, worth knowing before review:** the episode no longer ends on a win, so someone will eventually call it negative. The answer is panel 16 — the test is green and it took two messages. The report was right about him and it was useful to him, and the episode's position is that those are the same fact. If a reviewer wants the upbeat version, the change is panel 18, not the ending.

## ASSISTANT
Both work, but "ask it to remember" just writes the file for you — there's no separate remembering. In Claude Code, typing `#` at the start of a message adds a memory and asks which file to save it to; it then lands in one of these:

| Where | Scope | Use it for |
|---|---|---|
| `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` | **every chat, every project** | personal meta-rules — "no em dashes", "ask which approach before starting" |
| `./CLAUDE.md` at a repo root | everyone working in that repo, committed to git | project conventions, the AJ case |
| `./sub/dir/CLAUDE.md` | loaded when you're working in that subtree | rules that only apply to one area |
| `.claude/skills/*/SKILL.md` | fires when a task matches | multi-step workflows, not always-on rules |
| a hook in `.claude/settings.json` | runs every time, deterministically | things the model must not be able to skip |

On your machine right now: **you have no `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md`** — that's the one to create for a rule you want in every chat. `product-brain` has a project one (49 KB). `comics-video` has none.

```bash
echo '- When I say "whatever", ask which approach before you start.' >> ~/.claude/CLAUDE.md
```

Two things worth knowing:

**"Remember this" in this app is not the same as CLAUDE.md, and it's per-directory.** Your auto-memory lives in `~/.claude/projects/<cwd>/memory/`, and you already have two of them — one for `/Users/sejal` and one for `/Users/sejal/Documents/comics-video`. A memory saved in one does not surface in the other. For a rule that must hold everywhere, the user-level CLAUDE.md is the reliable one.

**CLAUDE.md is instructions, not enforcement.** The model reads it and usually complies; it can still miss it, and a rule buried at the bottom of a 49 KB file gets less attention than one near the top. That's why the real `/insights` reports say *where* in CLAUDE.md to add each suggestion and insist on actionable rules rather than guidelines. If a rule genuinely must never be skipped — formatting, a test gate — that's a hook, because the harness runs hooks, not the model.

For the script this all checks out: AJ's rule is about his repo's tests, so the project file is the right home, and panel 11's *"In the file. Then it holds for every session"* is accurate as written. If you'd rather the episode teach the everywhere version, the line becomes "In your CLAUDE.md. Then it holds in every project" — but I'd keep it project-scoped, since that's the one a viewer can paste into a repo their team shares.

## What changed and why

**Panel 8 now plants the whole episode.** "You are never slowed down by not knowing something" is real praise for a junior, and it is also the cause of panels 10, 12 and 14. The report compliments the exact trait that is ruining him, and it has no idea.

**Panel 12 is a habit, not a Tuesday.** Nine sessions, seven deletions. One incident is a bad day, seven is a thing he does, and that is why it is worth a section. No date, no single file, nothing he can dismiss as a one off.

**Panels 16 to 19 never cut away from the screen.** The shot is the monitor; AJ exists only as a reflection in it, which is what lets the suggestions fire without a reaction shot breaking the rhythm. His face changes three times in that reflection while the machine is politely helpful, and the fourth time he is not in it any more.

**The suggestions are all genuinely good, and that is the joke.** Each one is a real thing `/insights` outputs, each is derived from a finding we watched land, and the first three pay off panels 14, 12 and 10 in reverse order. Individually useful, stacked they are an indictment. The `and 11 more` counter is what breaks him, not any single line.

**Gone:** "One final suggestion to improve prompt quality," "Consider asking a colleague," every "sir," and every em dash in dialogue and cards.

## What his block changes

**The machine answers him exactly once, and this is it.** Everywhere else it reads sections and doesn't hear him. Panel 16 is the one line aimed at a thing AJ actually said, which is what makes it the turn rather than another card.

**"I can't pick for you. I can remember for you."** is the episode's thesis and it's six words. It also quietly corrects the thing most people get wrong about these tools.

**The Copy button is the get-started moment made literal.** A viewer watching this knows exactly what running it looks like, down to the grey `why:` line under each suggestion.

## Notes on the new ending

**Sameer never finishes the answer, and that's the loop.** "he ran…" turns into the question in one breath, so the cut lands inside a single continuous line of his. Record 24 and 25 as **one take** and split at "he ran…" — same rule as before, and it now costs one panel less than the three-line version.

**Panel 23's caption rule.** The capitals are baked-in emphasis on screen; the closed-caption band still carries the whole line, including the lowercase tail, per the caption system. The lowercase half is the audio bed that runs under panel 24 and is cut dead at the join to 25.

**"i couldn't care less" is the last thing he says and it is a lie.** He screamed thirty seconds ago and pressed Copy eleven times. Nobody flags it.

**Panel 22 pays off twice.** On its own, the viewer just watched "whatever" ding up in 41 sessions. Anyone who saw `graph` gets the exact incident — the four tasks, the all-nighter. That makes it a real cross-episode callback while staying self-contained, which is the only way the series allows one.

**"WAIT. YOU CAN DO THAT??" is EP001's line, reused.** I'd let it become a runner rather than treat it as a one-off. It's the same emotional beat both times — a person finding out the tool already did more than they thought — and that's the show's thesis, so it earns repeating.

## Flags

**⚠ `MEMORABLE MOMENT` still needs one live check.** It's in the March system prompt as *Fun Ending → the most memorable moment*, but I couldn't find a matching section in the current build's strings. Run `/insights` and see whether the last card is still there and what it's called before this gets drawn, because two panels hang off it.

**Panel 14's `SUGGESTION BOX` title still clashes with panel 16's `SUGGESTED CLAUDE.md ADDITIONS`.** Same note as before, still not actioned since you said touch nothing else. Retitling 14 as part of `WHERE THINGS GO WRONG` fixes it and is accurate to the real report.

**25 panels puts this at the top of the runtime cap.** If it comes in over two minutes, panel 10 is the cheapest cut: panel 12 does the same job harder, and losing it also loses one protest without hurting the rhythm.

## Three things that will bite

**Punctuate for generation, not for the caption.** AJ is written lowercase with no terminal punctuation because that's his caption style. Paste that into ElevenLabs and the lines run together with no breath. The list above is punctuated properly; the captions stay lowercase.

**Don't trust one tempo multiplier across the set.** Each generation picks its own delivery speed, so a take at the right atempo can still sound 30% slower than the one beside it. Measure syllables per second across his whole track and correct per take — that cost real time on EP003.

**Leave at least 0.4s of tail on the five cut takes** before you trim. Cutting tight to the last consonant is what makes a splice sound like a splice.

Panels 20, 22 and 25 are the AI and Sameer, so nothing of AJ's is missing from this list. Want Sameer's and Peter's next? Sameer's is the one with the split-take rule on it.

## ASSISTANT
Tags below are ElevenLabs v3 syntax. Two things to know before you paste: **tags only work on v3** — if a take comes back with the model actually saying "shouting", you're on v2 or Turbo — and **very short prompts are unstable on v3**, which is a problem when half his lines are four words. The grouped version further down solves that.

## Tagged takes

```
aj_p02
[casual][dismissive] Ohh, /insights? Nah nah, bet mine was worse.

aj_p03
[quiet][deadpan] I have no idea what that means.

aj_p04
[casual][curious] Yo, what's /insights?

aj_p06
[confident] Cool. Show me mine.

aj_p09
[pleased][smug] Fair, fair, I know, I pick stuff up fast.

aj_p11
[defensive] Hey, I don't do that, I read them.

aj_p13
[agitated][raising voice] Yo, yo, you need to calm down, man.

aj_p15
[frustrated][exasperated] I thought you were supposed to know all that and pick for me.

aj_p17
[hesitant][suspicious] ...So next time you just ask me?

aj_p21
[shouting][excited] WAIT. YOU CAN DO THAT??

aj_p23
[shouting] Okay okay that's enough, [muttering] I've had enough of this, you know what? I couldn't care less.

aj_bicker
[annoyed][muttering continuously] Nah but that's not, that's not even, I literally, no, because if you look at it, that's not what I, hang on, no...
```

## Grouped version, which is what I'd actually run

Twelve separate four-word requests will give you twelve different speaking rates and you'll spend the day fixing it. Generate these three blocks instead and cut at the line breaks — one request holds one delivery register, so the pace stays put.

```
BLOCK A — before the report turns on him (cut into p02, p03, p04, p06)
[casual][dismissive] Ohh, /insights? Nah nah, bet mine was worse. [quiet][deadpan] I have no idea what that means. [casual][curious] Yo, what's /insights? [confident] Cool. Show me mine.

BLOCK B — the four protests, escalating (cut into p09, p11, p13, p15)
[pleased][smug] Fair, fair, I know, I pick stuff up fast. [defensive] Hey, I don't do that, I read them. [agitated] Yo, yo, you need to calm down, man. [frustrated][exasperated] I thought you were supposed to know all that and pick for me.

BLOCK C — the turn and the outburst (cut into p17, p21, p23)
[hesitant][suspicious] ...So next time you just ask me? [shouting][excited] WAIT. YOU CAN DO THAT?? [shouting] Okay okay that's enough, [muttering] I've had enough of this, you know what? I couldn't care less.
```

Block B is the one that has to escalate — generate it in one go so the four protests climb against each other rather than each one starting from neutral.

## Settings and cuts

**Stability:** Natural for A and B. Creative for C, because `[shouting]` and the drop into `[muttering]` need the model to actually move — Creative follows tags harder at the cost of occasionally inventing delivery, so expect to regenerate C a couple of times.

**Still cut these five to the fragment, in the edit, not in the prompt:** p09 → `fair fair i know` · p11 → `hey i don't do that` · p13 → `yo yo you need to calm dow` · p15 → `…and pick for` · and panel 22 is the AI's, not his. Leave 0.4s of tail on each before trimming.

**p23 stays one take.** The capitals in the script are a caption decision; the audio never splits, and the lowercase half is the bed that runs under panel 24.

**`aj_bicker` should be generated long — 15 to 20 seconds — then filtered and cut to fit.** The words don't matter because it's unintelligible under the dialogue; what matters is that it never lands on a clean pause, or the ear will start trying to parse it.
